# Changes against witnesses C and H

The scans are authoritative. Rows count substantive local differences, not macro syntax or certainty alone. One location can differ from both witnesses, so the two witness totals overlap. Confidence describes the retained reading. All pixel boxes use the original scan coordinates.

H’s separate drawing files were not supplied. Figure comparisons use its positive local code, explicit claims and named label readings. Silence about a detail in a general figure description is not counted as an omission from the unavailable drawing. Documentary corrections to earlier comparison flags are preserved in the work records.

## Consequential corrections

- 142_scan101, Opening, after une action: une action de $\Theta$. The first line ends with action; the next main line starts de Θ. There is no theta or detached prime between those words, and the unused space at the right end contains no such symbol.
- 142_scan101, Upper margin, first Θ: $\Theta$ soit trivial. The oval crossed by its internal bar is immediately followed by soit; there is no upper-right prime.
- 142_scan101, Upper margin, exponent of the power: \Theta^{\unsure{\nu K}}. Above the power’s theta are a rounded nu-like stroke followed by an angular stem with two branches resembling K. The second character is not secure enough to remove the uncertainty.
- 142_scan101, Upper margin, after the power: de $\Theta$. A small joined de and a separate crossed oval follow the exponentiated theta in the slanted line.
- 142_scan101, Upper margin, sur I: sur $I$. The final noun is a capital I with top and bottom bars, following sur.
- 142_scan101, Upper margin, signs before connexe: $\unsure{+}$, $\unsure{=}$ connexe. A crossed long stem followed by comma is on the preceding line; two short near-parallel strokes precede connexe. Their linguistic value is unresolved, and the first may be dagger-like.
- 142_scan101, First main-text hypothesis, supplied Si: \deleted{$\unsure{\exists a\prime\in}$}. The line starts with the cancellation cluster, followed immediately by I′. No separately live Si is visible before or after the cluster. The possible older strokes inside the cluster remain represented only within the deletion. A possible Si-like trace inside the old cluster cannot be distinguished confidently from the deleted existential-sign strokes; what is not supported is a separately live Si.
- 142_scan101, Inserted condition-2 note, acting sign: \pi. The sign has one top bar and two descending legs, the right leg curved, with no oval enclosure and no single-legged Γ form.
- 142_scan101, Condition 3, displayed source basepoint: \widehat{\pi}_1(X',x). The small x after the comma is followed by a clear gap and closing parenthesis. Unlike the adjacent capital X′ and the preceding inline x′, no detached prime follows this x.
- 142_scan101, Lower margin, en supposant / ne supposant: en \unsure{en supposant\|ne supposant} \unsure{pas}. After the preceding en, two joined small strokes begin the next slanted line. They can be read en or ne; they do not securely establish ne. Following main-reader adjudication, en supposant is retained first and ne supposant as alternative. The grammatical attraction of a negative hypothesis does not decide the strokes.
- 142_scan101, Lower margin, pas after struck quantifier: \unsure{pas}. A short low written group follows the struck quantifier and precedes the next-line x′. Its initial descender and terminal loop admit pas, but overlap makes that reading uncertain.
- 142_scan101, Lower margin, membership I without prime: x'\in X'_K\cap I. The capital I has top and bottom bars followed directly by comma. There is no separate high prime before that comma; this differs from the plainly primed I′ in the main condition 3.
- 142_scan101, Lower margin, group acted on: \pi_1(X',x'). The π immediately after sur has its top bar and two legs with no separate hat above; the later target π explicitly carries a small roof.
- 142_scan101, Lower margin, kernel wording: \unsure{préserve le noyau de} $f$. The lower slanted lines retain a preserve-like run and a noyau-like n/o/y group with a descender, followed by de and a tall f. The full opening words remain doubtful rather than being supplied as certain prose.
- 142_scan101, Lower margin, f before final target: $f$. A distinct tall f with its long downward stroke and cross movement stands after de, before the final hatted fundamental-group expression. C omits this letter.
- 142_scan101, Condition 4, J definition and duplicate clause in H: que $J\overset{\mathrm{def}}=I'_1\cap f^{-1}(I'_2)\ne\phi$. The live line begins que J with a superscript def over its equality, then one intersection expression and ≠φ. There is only one such live expression; H separates and repeats it, and its pour has no separate source word.
- 142_scan101, Condition 4, source groupoid without hat: \Pi_1. The source capital Π has a broad straight cap and two straight legs; its top is immediately below the crossed prior formula, with no independent roof. The target’s roof is separately and plainly drawn.
- 142_scan102, Upper and right cancelled branch of the diagram: \diagram{\begin{tikzcd}[ampersand replacement=\&,row sep=3em,column sep=4em,<br>execute at end picture={<br>  \path (agA102.east) -- (agB102.west)<br>    coordinate[pos=.12] (agH102a) coordinate[pos=.35] (agH102b)<br>    coordinate[pos=.58] (agH102c) coordinate[pos=.82] (agH102d);<br>  \draw ([yshift=-.22em]agH102a) -- ([yshift=.32em]agH102b)<br>    -- ([yshift=-.22em]agH102b) -- ([yshift=.32em]agH102c)<br>    -- ([yshift=-.22em]agH102c) -- ([yshift=.32em]agH102d);<br>  \path (agB102.south) -- (agD102.north)<br>    coordinate[pos=.10] (agV102a) coordinate[pos=.32] (agV102b)<br>    coordinate[pos=.54] (agV102c) coordinate[pos=.76] (agV102d);<br>  \draw ([xshift=-.35em]agV102a) -- ([xshift=.35em]agV102a)<br>    -- ([xshift=-.35em]agV102b) -- ([xshift=.35em]agV102b)<br>    -- ([xshift=-.35em]agV102c) -- ([xshift=.35em]agV102c)<br>    -- ([xshift=-.35em]agV102d) -- ([xshift=.35em]agV102d);<br>}]<br>\|[alias=agA102]\| X'_1 \arrow[r,"\deleted{f_1}"] \arrow[dr,"f_1"] \&<br>\|[alias=agB102]\| \deleted{\unsure{Y_1}} \arrow[d,"\deleted{f}"] \\<br>X'_2 \arrow[r,"f_2"] \& \|[alias=agD102]\| Y_{\unsure{\deleted{2}\|2}}<br>\end{tikzcd}}. A horizontal arrow leaves X prime 1 toward an overwritten upper-right Y-shaped object. Its upper f and small 1 survive. A downward arrow and a right-side f survive beneath the large zigzag cancellation. C strikes only labels and the object; its shafts remain visually live. H omits the entire cancelled f_1 / Y_1 / f branch. The full proposed diagram draws cancellation across both shafts.
- 142_scan102, Diagram target: overworked subscript after Y: Y_{\unsure{\deleted{2}\|2}}. A small dark digit-like form sits to the lower right of Y. It resembles the hooked 2 in the neighboring f_2 and X prime 2, but has extra traversing strokes and a dark doubled baseline. Cancellation of an original 2 is the first reading; a heavily retraced live 2 remains possible. H loses the residual ink altogether.
- 142_scan103, Opening line, complex point space: X_{\mathbb C}(\mathbb C). A separate small C with a doubled/return stroke sits below the large X, before the parenthesized C. The subscript is not part of the following parenthesis.
- 142_scan103, Second line, interlinear morphism correction: \inserted{$-X$ un}. The interlinear shaft ends bluntly with a small thickened downward tip. It has no second slant forming an arrowhead, unlike the later x'→y' arrow. The X and un are above the cancelled phrase.
- 142_scan103, Cancelled Π₁ expression, arguments: \deleted{$\unsure{\widehat{\Pi}_1(X'_{\overline K},x',y')}$}. The large roof and two legs precede subscript 1. - 142_scan103, Formula (53), repaired left action sign: \deleted{\unsure{u}}{}^u l^T_{y',x'}. A heavily blackened small glyph precedes the surviving u, and the surviving u sits high to the left of l, above the l's baseline. The cancelled glyph's identity remains uncertain.
- 142_scan103, Membership display following (53), g's lower indices: g^{T\deleted{\unsure{u}}}_{x,y}(u). The lower line contains an x-like crossed hump, one separating dot/comma and y. There are no freestanding raised prime strokes after either lower letter, in contrast to the clearly written primes inside T's argument.
- 142_scan104, Crowded insertion after Donc, including its nested cancellation and final quantification: \inserted{si \deleted{\unsure{on peut}} la condition sur $x'_\alpha,y'_\alpha$ est vraie pour tt $\alpha$,}. The insertion starts si, includes a short struck on/peut-like run under the replacement la condition, then sur x prime alpha,y prime alpha est vraie. Beneath it the visible p descender and our ending precede two t strokes and alpha: pour tt alpha, not a standalone universal-quantifier sign. A curved attachment returns the whole insertion to after Donc.
- 142_scan104, Margin: script E after the functor name: $\mathcal E$. A separate curled capital E follows the quoted name. It differs visibly from the block E in the set definition, with a sweeping upper loop and curved lower stroke. H omits this symbol.
- 142_scan104, Margin: isolated I supplied by C: . No isolated mathematical I occurs between the closing-clause words. The ink belongs to the phrase sous-groupe ... de π noyau.

- 142_scan108, Lower sphere, upper right path label: $\eta$ relevé. The Greek letter has a left upright, an n-like arch, and a long right descender. Its two branches do not meet below the line as a gamma would. The word relevé and its terminal acute accent follow separately.

- 142_scan108, Lower sphere, downward head at the front vertical path’s i junction: \draw[<->,thick] (0.78,-0.56) .. controls (0.91,0.3) and (0.92,1.78) .. (0.57,2.48);. The source therefore has the additional local head at the i end of this upper segment; the consolidated/C code supplied only the northward end head. Only this segment becomes double-ended. - 142_scan110, Brace annotation, leading letter: \mathcal{E}. Connected curled upper/lower lobes match the three adjacent E signs. There is no angular Sigma zigzag. H explicitly doubts this letter in prose. Certainty removal from C is not itself counted.
- 142_scan110, Second diagram, left margin quotient: \mathcal{E}/\pi=\Gamma. The numerator has rounded curled lobes and a long lower flourish. The denominator has the angular roof and two pi legs; Gamma has one upright and a top cap.
- 142_scan110, Second diagram, upper-left object: \mathcal{E}(=\Gamma.\pi). The initial sign is the same double-lobed curled E as in the adjacent quotient. A dot separates Gamma and pi. The alphabetic reading, not dot syntax, is the discrepancy.

- Deleted superscript following T of g in membership line: \unsure{u}; alternatives: u; a different struck superscript letter. Two oblique strokes overprint the remaining letter body. Independent audit: the residual body remains crossed by two strong obliques; no specific alternative letter is imposed. (low).

**Still unresolved**

- Upper-square subgroup argument: \unsure{\Pi\|E}; alternatives: Pi; E. Roof/right-leg and E-arm strokes remain overlaid. (low).
- Stroke above/through brace: \unsure{2\|z}; alternatives: 2; z; part of the overwritten curled letter rather than an independent sign. Shape is entangled with the rewritten annotation. (low).
- Verb under brace: \unsure{opère\|opérant}; alternatives: opère; opérant. No secure final crossed t or full -ant sequence. (low).
- Sphere A crossed polar note: \deleted{\unsure{R\to a}}; alternatives: R to a. Small locally reversed letter/arrow cluster is crossed. (low).
- D+ caption first point: \unsure{0}; alternatives: 0. Oval overdrawn by upright and return. (medium).
- Sphere C axis label: \unsure{q}; alternatives: q. Hook and descender survive but upper bowl is not secure. (low).
- Sphere C upper R label, including deletion state: \unsure{R_\infty^-}; alternatives: R_infinity^- live; R_infinity^- deleted. Crossings coincide with retraced polar rim; no separate cancellation is isolated. (low).
- Cancelled sphere E left 0: \deleted{\unsure{0}}; alternatives: 0. Oval crossed by draft strokes. (medium).
- Cancelled sphere E adjacent looped-stem glyph: \deleted{\unsure{b\|6}}; alternatives: b; 6. Stem above small loop is entangled with crossing stroke. (low).

- cancelled lower omega labels in Q/P formulas: {}_{\deleted{\unsure{\omega}}}; alternatives: old omega index; unrecoverable portions of the old label. Repeated bowl remnants and matching upper replacements support omega, but dense hatching remains. (medium).
- first Q in NB box, cancelled lower inverse label: {}_{\deleted{\unsure{\omega^{-1}}}}; alternatives: omega^{-1}; partly obscured lower inverse label. The minus and upright 1 seem to survive at the end of the cancellation, in addition to omega. (medium).
- before racine: \deleted{$\unsure{\zeta}$}; alternatives: zeta-like cancelled symbol; another short crossed symbol or false start. The cluster is short and zigzagged, not a securely readable complete word. (low).
- after on prendra: \deleted{$\unsure{\zeta}$}; alternatives: zeta-like cancelled symbol; another short crossed symbol. A small cancellation is visible, but its underlying symbol is not secure. (low).

**Still unresolved**

- cancelled phrase after côté: \deleted{\unsure{du plus proche}}; alternatives: du plus proche; partly obscured cancelled wording. Crossing line obscures several low letters; initial du and p/l/pro sequence remain. (medium).
- cancelled phrase before au sommet: \deleted{\illegible{1} \unsure{repère} opposé}; alternatives: one unreadable word followed by repère opposé; middle cancelled word not fully secure. First word is physically unreadable; p descender and accent favour repère, with residual cancellation interference. (medium).
- (9), cancelled index cluster: \deleted{\unsure{+2\|i+2}}; alternatives: +2; i+2. The live i is independently clear to the left; black overwriting obscures whether the cancelled cluster also contains an old i. (medium).
- raised phrase after orientation dash: \inserted{\unsure{de = désignent}}; alternatives: de = désignent; compressed opening/ending not entirely secure. Visible d/e opening and g/nent ending favour the proposed reading, but the phrase is compressed and revised. (medium).
- live word above cancelled sont: \inserted{\unsure{sont\|des}}; alternatives: sont; des. Only one short added word is visible; the strokes do not securely decide which of these readings it carries. (low).
- Cela ... semble, cancelled word: \deleted{\unsure{nous}}; alternatives: nous; short cancelled pronoun not fully secure. The strike obscures the n/ou sequence. (medium).
- second diagonal NB, cancelled word after les: \deleted{\unsure{ces}}; alternatives: ces; obscured short cancelled word. The visible low c/e and final hook fit ces, but are crossed. (medium).
- attachment of last two marginal physical groups: si on bouche\\ R_r^i,\quad;\quad(\uline{NB} si\\ les disques où\\ \lambda_r^i\ \text{devient}=1); alternatives: two separate unfinished/abbreviated notes; interleaved continuation with an unresolved reading order. The second diagonal block visibly ends with si on bouche / R_r^i, while the separate lower block begins (NB si and continues les disques où / lambda_r^i devient =1). Both si tokens are present. Their physical grouping is clear, but an intended interleaved reading order is not; the two groups remain separate in the TeX. (low).
- final paragraph, double-contoured C: \unsure{\mathbb{C}\|\mathcal{C}}; alternatives: \mathbb{C}; \mathcal{C}. The outer and inner C-like contours are independently visible. They support the preserved doubled rendering but do not determine whether the intended letter is blackboard C or calligraphic C; the analytic context does not settle the ink. (medium).
- cancelled word before portions: \deleted{\unsure{une}}; alternatives: une; obscured short cancelled word. The short low-letter run fits une, but the cancellation merges its middle and final strokes; the enlarged existing orientation crop does not justify removing the uncertainty. (medium).

- Opening exponent, stroke after right star: \unsure{\vcenter{\hbox{\tikz[x=1mm,y=1mm]{\draw[line width=.4pt] (0,0)--(.04,.85);}}}}; alternatives: prime-like separate stroke; detached or retraced part of star. The upright ink is distinct but its semantic attachment is not secure; it is preserved as a graphic rather than a prime. (low).
- Middle tilde-X variant, upper-left mark: \unsure{\cdot\|*}; alternatives: \cdot; *. A dense roughly round center favors a point, but short crossing strokes may belong to an overworked star. (medium).
- Detached fragment below the dash in On notera sentence: Uncertain small graphical loop/angular fragment; alternatives: unidentified live fragment; unidentified retraced fragment. No word or numeral can be isolated; no independent cancellation stroke establishes deletion. (low).
- Analytic parenthesis: \unsure{et $=$\|$\ne$}; alternatives: et followed by the même abbreviation; not-equal sign. Two horizontal strokes survive and a lower-left looped/angular glyph favors et; no certain slash through the upper bar. (medium).
- Triangle, lower/right end of 0-corner arc: Dense angular endpoint contour retained; alternatives: second downward/right head; thick retracing of the arc endpoint. The wedge is visible but its precise directional function is not secure; no second directed edge is asserted. (low).
- Triangle, cluster below the live 1: Uncertain small graphical ink cluster; alternatives: earlier infinity; earlier zero; unidentified overwritten mark. Loop openings survive within dense crossing strokes; both glyph identity and deletion/overwriting state are uncertain. (low).
- Final groupoid family opening delimiter: \unsure{\{\|(}; alternatives: \{; (. A left-facing middle spur favors a brace, but the glyph is heavily retraced. Both source closers remain round. (medium).

**Still unresolved**

- opening cancelled wording: \deleted{\unsure{soit la\|qui est la}}; alternatives: soit la; qui est la. The long strike obscures the short internal letters. (low).
- first display cancelled mathematics: \deleted{\unsure{\tau_0\tau_\infty\|\tau_0}}; alternatives: \tau_0\tau_\infty; \tau_0. Two glyph clusters are favored, but cancellation is heavy. (medium).
- cancelled phrase before sens défini: \deleted{\unsure{demi-tour} \unsure{indiqué\|défini}}; alternatives: demi-tour with obscured internal letters; indiqué; défini. Strike and replacement strokes overlap the old letters. (medium).
- both connectors in(13): \mathrel{\tikz[baseline=-.5ex]{\draw[line width=.45pt] (0,0)--(.6,0)--(.6,.07);}}; alternatives: unheaded connector with terminal pen flick; rapid arrow whose intended head is not separately recoverable. The shaft-plus-tick geometry is certain, but intent is not recoverable from shape alone. (medium).
- drawing, lower transformed R second-index position: R_{\tau_1r}^{0\unsure{\tikz[x=.045cm,y=.045cm,baseline=-.4ex]{\draw[line width=.45pt] (0,0)--(.8,2)--(1.8,.3)--(.2,1.5)--(2,1.3);}}}; alternatives: infinity-shaped index remnant; 1-shaped index remnant; endpoint overwork without a separable second index. Only zero can be securely separated from the thick neighboring endpoint. (low).
- drawing, cancelled prefix before upper transformed R: \deleted{\unsure{\Lambda}}; alternatives: Lambda-shaped cancelled glyph; unrecoverable angular residue. Crossing ink obscures the original angular character. (medium).
- drawing, additional residue beside the upper return arrow: \unsure{\tikz[x=.055cm,y=.055cm,baseline=-.5ex]{\draw[line width=.6pt] (0,0)--(1,2.7)--(2.8,.1)--(.3,1.6)--(3,1.8);\draw[line width=.6pt] (.2,2.4)--(2.5,.2) (1.1,-.2)--(2.1,2.4);}}; alternatives: cancelled label fragment; overworked earlier graphic stroke. Separate crosshatched residue is physically certain; no glyph or intended path can be identified. (low).
- drawing, upper endpoint of lambda_r^1: \unsure{\blacktriangle} at the upper endpoint; alternatives: narrow upward/right arrowhead; endpoint thickening. The cap/wing favors a head, but the endpoint is thickened. (medium).
- Fine oblique trace at the first (14) frame’s lower side: \draw[line width=.18pt] ([xshift=15pt,yshift=-1.4pt]eq14a.south)<br>  -- ([xshift=19pt,yshift=4.6pt]eq14a.south);; alternatives: incidental pen or pencil trace; unidentified purposeful graphic trace. The physical fine rising-right stroke is visible and copied, but its origin and intended function cannot be recovered. No head, glyph, graph edge or mathematical cancellation is assigned. (medium).
- Drawing, upper pencil junction near infinity: \draw[gray] (G)--(2.70,4.26)<br>  .. controls (2.78,4.25) and (2.81,4.18) .. (2.76,4.12);<br>\draw[gray!45,thin] (2.76,4.12)<br>  .. controls (2.83,4.10) and (2.88,4.19) .. (C);; alternatives: a continuous but faint or retraced intervening contour; separate provisional pencil strokes whose continuity is not established. The downturned terminal hook and faint intervening trace are visible; the latter does not securely establish whether the incoming and outgoing pencil segments were intended as one joined contour. (medium).

**Still unresolved**

- Second row of (15), cancelled condition: \deleted{\unsure{i\in\{0,\infty\}}}; alternatives: Exact membership/brace strokes partly masked. Diagonal cancellation passes through several of the small signs. (medium).
- Relations paragraph, final word before revision: \unsure{redondances\|redondance}; alternatives: redondances; redondance. The terminal down-and-return stroke can be an s but is close to the revision hook. (medium).
- Cancelled (16), first Lambda indices: \Lambda_{\unsure{\tau_1r}}^{\unsure{\infty\|\prime\infty}}; alternatives: \tau_1r; \infty; \prime\infty. The slashing strokes obscure part of the lower index and any detached prime beside the infinity. No new character is invented. (medium).
- Cancelled NB, earlier replaced word: \deleted{\unsure{inverse}}; alternatives: inverse. The word is crossed independently and then included in the later block cancellation. (medium).
- Large tiling, lower-left small path label: \unsure{\lambda^0\|\Lambda^0}; alternatives: \lambda^0; \Lambda^0. The curved descending initial stroke favors lowercase, but the short return allows a capital reading. (medium).
- Large tiling, right endpoint of thick D--C shaft: Leftward shaft with a filled right endpoint; alternatives: The filled endpoint may conceal an additional reverse head. A left-facing tip is separate; the right mass has no separable outward tip. (medium).
- Large tiling, right endpoint of thick A--B shaft: Leftward shaft with a filled right endpoint; alternatives: The filled endpoint may conceal an additional reverse head. The right ink mass joins the downward curve’s head; its components cannot be separated securely. (medium).
- Large tiling, short horizontal beside H--B: Leftward arrow with rounded filled right point; alternatives: A reverse head may be merged into the rounded point. Only the left tip is independently pointed. (medium).
- Large tiling, thick lower return about central infinity: Curved return with heads at its two ends; alternatives: Two successive directed arcs as C depicts. The crowded dark ink makes the precise separation of endpoints and any intermediate head less secure. (medium).
- Large tiling, small loop beside tau_1(r): The visible open loop reproduced as an unlabelled ink stroke; alternatives: An earlier symbol; A retraced initial stroke; An independent small mark. The loop is visible but its function and deletion status are not established. (medium).
- Live (16), outer exponent on first group: \unsure{+\|-1}; alternatives: +; -1. Overwritten horizontal and upright strokes permit both readings; the algebraic base-word check does not decide it. (low).
- Live (16), cancelled exponent beside first beta annotation: \beta_{\tau_1(r)}^{1\,\deleted{\unsure{-1}}}; alternatives: -1. The small crossed cluster retains a dash and upright but is partly masked. Its raised position beside the live superscript 1 is clear; the uncertain deleted reading is preserved there. (medium).
- Cancelled du type relation, tall stroke immediately before Lambda_r^0=1: \unsure{\vcenter{\hbox{%<br>\begin{tikzpicture}[x=.55cm,y=.55cm]<br>\draw[line width=.45pt] (0.02,.65)..controls(0.03,.48)and(0.06,.30)..(0.015,.23)<br> ..controls(-.02,.17)and(-.13,.20)..(-.12,.12)<br> ..controls(-.10,.06)and(.07,.07)..(.08,-.04)<br> ..controls(.10,-.12)and(.06,-.25)..(.13,-.26)--(.22,-.24);<br>\end{tikzpicture}}}}; alternatives: Brace-like graphic; Bent earlier stroke traversed by the cancellation. The tall vertical, leftward middle bulge and lower right hook are visible, but its formal role is not established. No matching brace or lexical completion is supplied. (medium).

**Still unresolved**

- Cancelled enfin: \unsure{enfin}; alternatives: enfin; partly unresolved initial strokes plus fini-like tail. The short initial strokes and cancellation make the full word uncertain. (medium).
- Cancelled pour: \unsure{pour}; alternatives: pour; unresolved short p-word. The strike masks the middle letter junctions. (medium).
- Long cancelled index condition: \unsure{i,j\ne\infty}; alternatives: i,j\ne\infty; partly unresolved index/relation cluster. Several sign strokes merge with the cancellation. (medium).
- Long cancelled verbal continuation: \unsure{les côtés ne passent pas par $\infty$}; alternatives: preferred phrase as given; partly unreadable words under the strike. Only portions of the letter sequence and the final infinity/parenthesis remain independently separable. (low).
- First source in (18): \unsure{R_r^{01}\|R_r^{0,1}}; alternatives: 01; 0,1. Possible comma lies in the beginning of the cancellation hatching. (medium).
- Cancelled old first-row arrow/letter in (18): \unsure{\to R}; alternatives: arrow plus R; partly unresolved arrow/looped capital. Oblique hatching masks the arrowhead and lower part of the letter. (medium).
- Cancelled Lambda in (18): \unsure{,}\deleted{\unsure{\Lambda_r^{\prime1}\|\Lambda_r^1}}; alternatives: prime1 with comma before; unprimed1 and/or no separate comma. Potential prime and low comma-like stroke are entangled with hatching. (medium).
- Second target in (18): \unsure{R_{\tau_1(r)}^{01}\|R_{\tau_1r}^{01},}; alternatives: parenthesized r; unparenthesized r followed by comma. Small opening hook and final curved stroke cannot be separated fully from r/punctuation. (medium).
- Third target in (18): \unsure{R_{\tau_0(r)}^{1,0}\|R_{\tau_0r}^{1,0},}; alternatives: parenthesized r; unparenthesized r followed by comma. Same local curved-hook ambiguity as the preceding row. (medium).
- Drawing upper-right endpoint label: \unsure{1}; alternatives: 1; unresolved retraced angular/looped glyph. Ordinary1 strokes are not secure; surrounding type labels are not used to supply the numeral. (low).
- Drawing central-right overworked glyph: uncertain small graphic; alternatives: 1/diamond-like label; overwritten unresolved mark. The body is entangled with overtracing just above the horizontal hatched edge. (low).
- Tiny tick below the final lowest star: uncertain short horizontal graphic; alternatives: detached star stroke; unidentified small tick. Its existence is secure, but it is not assigned a separate mathematical role. (low).
- First-row replacement connector in (18): intention of the capped terminal: \underset{\vcenter{\hbox{\begin{tikzpicture}[x=1mm,y=1mm]<br>\draw[line width=.4pt] (0,0)..controls(2,-.15)and(5,.12)..(7,.12);<br>\draw[line width=.5pt] (7,-.35)--(7,.62);<br>\end{tikzpicture}}}}{\deleted{\unsure{\to R}}}; alternatives: literal lower shaft with short cap, as drawn; intended right arrow with its head obscured by the old R contour. The physical cap is definite; the overlap prevents identification of a separate conventional head. No altered mathematical map is inferred. (medium).
- Low caption stroke after the dash and before les: \raisebox{-.45ex}{$\unsure{\vcenter{\hbox{\tikz[x=1mm,y=1mm]{\draw[line width=.35pt] (0,0)--(.65,.4);}}}}$}; alternatives: comma-like punctuation; incidental short pen stroke. The separate rising-right trace is visible, but its function cannot be fixed from its placement alone. (medium).

**Still unresolved**

- Opening cancelled R-label: \deleted{$\unsure{R_\varphi\|R_q}$}; alternatives: R_\varphi; R_q. The cancellation partly covers the low index, which has both a loop and a descending hook. (low).
- Interlinear phrase after liés: \unsure{d'indice\|d'arc}; alternatives: d’indice; d’arc. Compressed linked letters cannot be separated securely after the visible d/apostrophe; final infinity is secure. (low).
- First Lambda subscript in first relation(22): \unsure{\sigma\|r}; alternatives: \sigma; r. Closed low loop and long upper stroke favor sigma, but an unusually looped r cannot be excluded; both witnesses gave unmarked r. (medium).
- Hole label in lower paragraph: \unsure{\varphi s\|s}; alternatives: \varphi s; s with phi-like preceding overwork cancelled. A separate s follows the heavily retraced phi-like glyph; no independent cancellation stroke establishes the state of that first component. (medium).

**Still unresolved**

- End of upper line in the closing paragraph: \unsure{autom}; alternatives: autom, preferred; an incompletely separable short abbreviation; no unsupported full word supplied. Compressed small letters around the tall t-like stroke are not individually secure; the visible extent does not justify H's full expansion. (medium).
- Final word at the clipped lower edge: \unsure{Soulignons}; alternatives: Soulignons, preferred; remaining lower letter strokes cannot be securely recovered from the clipped scan. The initial and upper letter sequence support Soulignons, but the lower gnons region touches the scan edge. No alternative heading/colon is independently visible. (medium).
- Far-right extent of the xi-condition insertion leader: Definite long lower sweep with descending left attachment; no shaped right terminal supplied; alternatives: thin grey continuation belongs to the leader to the page edge; last continuation is partly paper crease and its exact ink endpoint cannot be separated. The narrow uncovered-tip crop shows the continuation at the paper edge but does not distinguish a final ink endpoint from the grey crease. The words and left attachment are secure. (medium).

**Still unresolved**

- Cancelled old subscript inside Hom in (11): \deleted{\unsure{\overline X}}; alternatives: A barred X is possible; exact older strokes are not securely recoverable under the hatching.. Repeated dense crossing strokes obscure the upper line and much of the former X-like body. The new X beneath is separate and secure. (low).
- Subgroup index after (13): \unsure{X',X\|X;X}; alternatives: X',X; X;X. An elongated upper mark and lower comma can be prime-plus-comma or a single semicolon. No additional dot permits a secure prime-plus-semicolon reading. (medium).
- Prefix of raised final refinement: \unsure{--\|ou}; alternatives: --; ou. A small compact mark and longer low stroke permit a broken dash or compressed two-letter word. (medium).
- Ending of précisément in the raised refinement: précisém\unsure{ent\|t}; alternatives: précisément; précisémt. The common prefix is readable; final low strokes are compressed and crowded by the descender from par above, so the full ending cannot be distinguished securely from an abbreviated terminal t. (medium).
